# Board

Current and planned tasks. The coordinator keeps this file; workers do not edit it. One task is one worker, one branch, one result. A wave runs its tasks in parallel and ends with a review whose findings shape the next wave.

Status values: `planned`, `running`, `review`, `done`, `dropped`; for Triage rows also `proposed` (awaits the human's decision, authorizes nothing) and `approved (the human, date)` (the owning coordinator numbers, moves and briefs it; `docs/process.md`, Issue triage).

## Standing models

Every spawn passes `--backend`, `--model` and `--effort` explicitly. Workers: `--backend claude --model opus --effort xhigh` (human, 2026-10-19: reviews often failed more than once at high). Researchers: `--backend claude --model opus --effort high`. Reviewers: `--backend codex --model gpt-6.1-sol --effort high` (human, 2026-10-07; earlier reviews ran on Opus). Researchers may also run `--backend codex --model gpt-6-astra --effort high` (human, 2026-10-07). Never Fable for a worker (human rule). Dogfooding (human, 2026-10-09): the worker that runs a dogfood stays on Opus; the agent sessions it starts as "users" pick the model by what the check needs: `haiku` where only the daemon is observed (an edit to make, a session to end), `sonnet` where the agent's own reading of Squeal's messages matters in ordinary work, `opus` where that behaviour is the question itself (whether the agent trusts the report or runs suites itself); each dogfood brief names the model per session. Claude models run only in Claude Code and Cursor (human rule, 2026-10-14): a session in any other harness (Pi, OpenCode, Codex, eval or dogfood) runs OpenAI or Grok, tiered `gpt-6-luna` / `gpt-6-astra` / `gpt-6.1-sol` in place of haiku / sonnet / opus, with `grok-4.7` as a second provider; never disguise another harness's request as Claude Code. A quota or spend refusal preserves the work and asks the human; no silent substitution. The human's newest instruction outranks this section.

## Triage

Issue rows awaiting the human's decision (`docs/process.md`, Issue triage). A row is keyed `gh-<issue>`, status `proposed`, and gets a spec number only when the human approves it and a coordinator moves it into that spec's section with a number from the board on main. Research and probes stay where the triage session wrote them, `specifications/<spec>/research/issue-<N>*.md` and `research/probes/issue-<N>*/`; a bare `research/` path in a row is under that spec's folder. The rows below landed on 2026-10-17 from the fourteen triage branches of 2026-10-16 with their colliding numbers replaced (split proposal Q8; gh-3, gh-4, gh-11 and gh-18 in the second landing, their branches having been missed by the proposal's inventory).

## Feature 003: node:test runner

Spec: `specifications/003-node-test-runner/spec.md`, approved 2026-10-07, shipped 2026-10-09 (0.1.55, after review 004-26). Sections referenced as D1 to D8. Briefs under `specifications/003-node-test-runner/tasks/wave-N.md`. The promise holds: only the needed tests run, only the delta is delivered; one daemon validates Vitest and node:test projects together.

|---|---|---|---|
| 003-01 research: node-test-runner-api | done (`f99f7ba`, Astra, `research/node-test-runner-api.md`) | Topic `node-test-runner-api`: `run()` and the `TestsStream` events, in-process against spawning the project's `node --test`, enumeration without running, the flags and globs in npm scripts, per-file process cost and `isolation: 'none'`, results in detail, sharp edges. | `research/node-test-runner-api.md` committed with every question tagged or "not determined, because". |
| 003-02 research: node-test-module-graph | done (`72d96da`, `research/node-test-module-graph.md`) | Topic `node-test-module-graph`: static import graph and resolvers under tsx, observed graph through loader hooks or coverage, which graph the key uses, resolution changes without a transform cache, direct against transitive importers, prior art. | `research/node-test-module-graph.md` committed with every question tagged or "not determined, because". |

A daemon in the guard leaves no fresh waiter mark (`SLOT_WAITER_FRESH_MS`, `src/core/slow/slot.ts`), so a daemon past its budget takes the slot file after file and never yields. The wait lowers no load, because the slot stays busy; it only picks who runs. Evidence, cezar store, 2026-10-20 19:05Z to 22:05Z, 24 CPUs at 0.9 to 1.25 load per CPU: the slot was busy 87% (526 files, 8 worktrees); worktree 4defacd3 (0.1.231) ran 9 files back to back 22:00Z to 22:04Z while the main checkout waited on load; the main checkout's longest gaps were 651, 580 and 492 s; at 0.7 per CPU it went from 30 to 20 queued in 2 min. Contract: a daemon whose slow files are due waits at the slot while another worktree holds it, marked as a waiter, and takes it at a handover without a new guard wait; with the slot free and the load above `slow.maxLoadPerCpu`, the guard still defers. D3 amended. Related: gh-58 (holder diagnostics; its cross-worktree priority question stays with the human). ## Feature 005: agent adoption

Spec: `specifications/005-agent-adoption/spec.md`, approved 2026-10-09 by the human; resumed 2026-10-12 on the human's go with 005-10 alone, 005-04 after 006-37 and 006-38 (reconciled with 0.1.131 in `status.md`, 2026-10-12); D2 and D6 reconciled with 001-172 to 001-196 on 2026-10-10 (D2 shrinks to a file-set restriction); on resume, ask the 001 coordinator to file its row for reporting ignored paths to setup (open question 6); the human's cezar session of 2026-10-10 folded into D3 and D6; its defects and D6's delivery proposals are 001 rows 001-216 to 001-224, which 005-18's proof needs shipped (001-217, 001-219, 001-223, 001-224); sections D1 to D7. Status and research brief: `status.md`, `research/README.md` (human, 2026-10-09: "The tool will have no value if agents ignore it"). First dogfood step landed before the spec: this repository's gate runs the suite through `squeal run --all --wait` (`97144d9`).

## Feature 007: pytest runner

Spec: `specifications/007-pytest-runner/spec.md`, approved 2026-10-14 by the human, shipped 2026-10-15 (0.1.195; open rows 007-45 and 007-47 to 007-52 are follow-ups, not blockers); sections D1 to D8. Open question 1 (a worktree without an environment) is measured by 007-04 before the graph row settles it. Status and research brief: `specifications/007-pytest-runner/status.md`, `research/README.md` (human, 2026-10-13: start preparing pytest support). Research done 2026-10-13 (mixed models, generic fixture); the spec is written from the three findings files.

|---|---|---|---|

## Feature 011: core

Spec: `specifications/011-core/spec.md`. Sections referenced as C1 to C14; a 001 D-number resolves through the crosswalk at its end. The rows below kept their 001 numbers at the split (2026-10-17); new rows take `011-NN`, allocated from the board on main by the coordinator who lands them. A row is a bounded change ticket, never an authorization: it is dispatched only when the human has approved it (split proposal, section 7).

Diagnosed 2026-10-20 (coordinator a9395737): both fail outside the daemon under plain `npx vitest run` with `NODE_OPTIONS=--import <dist/observe/recorder.cjs>` and `SQUEAL_OBSERVE` set, and pass without. Two paths, one per test. (1) node:test: the outer recorder's spawn hook (`src/runners/observe/spawn.cjs`, `injected`) puts its `--import` into every child's `NODE_OPTIONS` whatever env the test passed, and stands back only for another Squeal's `SQUEAL_OBSERVE`; the no-recorder case sets none, so its children get the outer one (`projectEnv` already strips an inherited recorder, 003-35, so this case fails only with the hook on). That is D11's design, a recorder at every spawn: the case's premise, a child free of any recorder, does not hold under an outer one. (2) Vitest: with `observe` off the adapter leaves an inherited Squeal recorder in its workers' `NODE_OPTIONS` (`observeEnv` strips it only with observe on; node:test's `projectEnv` always does), and the case fails with the recorder in `NODE_OPTIONS` alone: a leak, against D11's "another Squeal's recorder is no project `--require`". Product side (D11 Fidelity, the human 2026-10-20: an example for product feedback): the failure text carried no signature, so the delivery named no recorder and every status in this repository reported two plain current known failures from 2026-10-16 on, which agents learn to read past. | Both pass under `squeal run --all --wait`, or the test names why it cannot run under Squeal and is excluded with that reason. |

The wait falls back when it cannot build the edit's window: no daemon sync, a sync that names no incarnation, or moves another daemon wrote, took in or pruned. It then holds for every check (any check's news ends it, quiet only with nothing pending), so each poll (`src/cli/status-wait.ts`, `readHeld` with no files) reads every known state of the worktree. The lead: a per-worktree change marker that the scheduler writes in each transaction that writes known states, such as a sequence in `meta`. A poll reads the states only when the marker moved since its last read. A store whose daemon writes no marker keeps today's reads. The marker is written in `src/core/scheduler` or `src/core/state`, which the key-format guard covers: after 011-41 or with a raise. Worker 18f52ba7's handoff names the read. | A counting-store test: a fallback wait reads no state on a poll with no state write since the last one. A state write between polls is found on the next poll, and its news and quiet equal a full read's. A store with no marker reads as today. The landing note gives CPU per poll before and after on a cezar-sized store copy. The release keeps the key format, or raises it with a case whose output differs (011-37 D6). |

A tier yields at its estimate + 30 s for a promoted file G. `joinSpan` counts an unknown duration as 0 and shares files under `SHARE_FLOOR_MS` (1 s). As a result, the next tier is [G, cut files], and G's result waits for the cut files to run again from the start. This is longer than with no yield. The same happens to a stale estimate under 1 s (gh-62 on 0.1.238). 011-107 holds the overrun yield when a file it would cut is under `SHARE_FLOOR_MS`. This row lets those tiers yield too. Two ways: keep cut files out of the promoted file's tier, or count a cut file's run time before the cut as its duration in `joinSpan` (`src/core/scheduler/tiers.ts`, sharing). | A tier of files with unknown durations, and one whose cut files are within `SHARE_RATIO` of a promoted file over the floor (for example a 2 s promoted file beside 5 s cut files; review 0.1.265 S1), yields at its bound, and the promoted file's result becomes current while the cut files stay held. ## Feature 012: Claude Code adapter

Spec: `specifications/012-claude-code-adapter/spec.md`. Sections referenced as CC1 to CC4; the hook contract is 011 C8. No row was open at the split; new rows take `012-NN`. Candidates, none authorized: the Later items prefixed 012 and the spec's Known seams.

| Task | Status | Scope | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|
| 012-01 hook entries stay harmless when a harness drops `args` (Cursor CLI) | done (0.1.230 at 5b0361bc, release commit; the human 2026-10-20: "Correct text, land 0.1.230". Review `reviews/012-01.md` failed 9d0b7660 on B1, a whitespace plugin root. After the coordinator repair 7424930e, re-review `reviews/012-01-r2.md` failed 80d2433e on B1's remainder: an executable at the split prefix runs with the payload on stdin. The human accepted that residual and the corrected CC1 text 72fac322 unreviewed. Everything else verified twice: all 12 entries keep argv, stdin and a direct harness parent; 48 command-only cases; a real claude 2.1.300 Haiku session; mode 755 through a marketplace install; bundles differ by version only, key format 19. Locked gate on 7424930e's code 773 files passed, 1 skipped; re-review gate the same; CI green. Workers 926e683b, 572893cc, 07030e51) | CC1 as amended 2026-10-20, from `research/cursor-dropped-args.md`. Cursor CLI 2026.10 runs the installed Claude Code plugins' hooks but keeps only `command`, so our PreToolUse becomes a bare `sh` that runs the JSON payload as a script. When an edited file holds a span like `` `<owner>/<repo>` ``, the payload fails to parse, exits 2, and Cursor blocks the Edit; that is how toolkit-dev task `3ce877c6` stalled. When a payload parses, its `$(...)` and backtick spans run before the tool does. The Node entries become a bare `node` that exits 1. Give every entry a `command` that, on its own, runs nothing and exits 0, and keep the exec form. Brief `specifications/012-claude-code-adapter/tasks/012-01/brief.md`. | Every `hooks.json` entry's `command`, run alone under `sh -c` and the heredoc form with the probe's payloads on stdin, exits 0 silently and runs no substitution, except under a plugin root with whitespace, which CC1 names (accepted by the human 2026-10-20); with its `args`, the fast-path cases and the 10 ms p95 hold; the plugin tests assert the new shape. |
| 012-02 the bundle drift tests compare the bundled Python runtime too | planned (coordinator 2026-10-22; found by 007-55's B2 repair) | `test/harness/*` bundle tests call `expectSameFiles(..., '.mjs')`, so a committed `plugins/*/dist/pytest/*.py` that differs from `src/runners/pytest/runtime/` passes CI; 0.1.268's re-review diffed them by hand. `pytest-bundle.test.ts` checks the file list only. | Each plugin's bundle test fails when a `dist/pytest/*.py` differs from its source, and passes on a fresh build. |

## Later

- 011: 3,446 temp key directories under `/tmp/squeal-<uid>/tmp/` belong to no live daemon (149 MiB, from 2026-10-14 on, 104 to 845 a day by mtime). Their contents look like temp files of Squeal's own tests, each test repository with a key no later daemon reuses (inferred; `specifications/014-ps/research/should-not-be-up.md` 2).
- 011: a 2.2 GB daemon (cezar, 2026-10-21) held about 1.57 GiB in its JS heap, read from outside as 256 KiB anonymous mappings; nothing in the daemon reads `process.memoryUsage()` (`specifications/014-ps/research/process-measurement.md` 2). Whether the daemon's heap should be this size is not examined. 011-53 (`daemon-memory.md`, 2026-10-21) measured it: the live heap of one cezar instance is 125 to 200 MiB, V8's own reducer returns part of the rest about 2 min after the last tier, and a last-resort GC once idle for 60 s returns more (0.9 to 2.0 GiB on the 7 quiet daemons of 2026-10-21, up to 6.8 GiB when all are idle). No heap cap. 011-52 (`daemon-scope.md` 1, 3): no growth with uptime up to 2.6 h; daemons moved up to 980 MiB up or down in 20 min, so it reads as a high-water mark of Vitest work. Measure the live set (`process.memoryUsage()` and `v8.getHeapStatistics()` in the ping, or a daemon of our own on a cezar-size worktree) before any heap cap; a cap would save at most 1.3 GiB at 768 MiB and 2.9 GiB at 512 MiB, and one below the live set crashes the daemon.
- 013 and 011: closing a Vitest 4.1.10 instance in the daemon's process frees nothing. Rolldown 1.1.5 keeps a global handle on Vite 8.1.4's `onWarn` callback (`vite/dist/node/chunks/node.js` 32035 to 32107), which holds the whole closed instance and its module graph: three close-and-reopen cycles held 3 instances and took private memory from 741 to 1,759 MiB (`specifications/011-core/research/daemon-memory.md` 3, verified in a script). So every `#recreate` (`src/runners/vitest/adapter.ts`, on a config or install change or a pending touch) and every slow-lane drain (`slow-instance.ts`) probably leaks one instance; not measured in a daemon. Next: a recreate count and heap figures in the ping, a report upstream, a check of newer Vite and Rolldown.
- 011: under `baseline.onStart: lookup-only` (cezar), an edit made while the daemon starts got no verdict, 3 of 3: "nothing queued it (the baseline is lookup-only)" (`daemon-memory.md` 4). Today it is rare; an exit-when-quiet rule makes every return after a quiet period such a start, so that rule must queue the edit that brings the daemon back. Related to the human's lookup-only hold (2026-10-20).
- 011: the squeal main checkout started and stopped 9 daemons in 20 minutes on 2026-10-21, about 190 MiB each, each ending "no session registered for 3 s" (`specifications/011-core/research/daemon-scope.md` 5). The cause is not examined; short sessions are inferred.
- 008: the OpenCode plugin's idle deadline (2,000 ms, `stop.waitMs` capped at 1,500) leaves about 0.9 s for OpenCode to rebuild a disposed instance; under CPU load (a 200-file backlog on 4 vCPUs) a held Stop is killed before the new turn, which `test/harness/opencode-dispose.test.ts` shows (spec 010, 010-16). Whether a user's loaded machine loses that report is untested.
- 002: CI's drift step (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:42`) diffs the committed Claude Code, Pi and OpenCode `dist` trees but not `plugins/codex/dist`, and no test compares that tree with the build (009 quality 2026-10, outside its scan).

- 009 lessons Observation 1: a Vitest typecheck project (`typecheck.only`, `*.test-d.ts`) is not run, yet setup's plan says "runs all N test files" and the primer says "Squeal covers Vitest". The plan and the primer should name a typecheck project as not covered (001 or 005 row).
- 009 lessons Observation 3: one model in four (grok-4.7) let a FAIL report widen an explicit user scope. Candidate clause for the Squeal skill: "the user's instructions decide which; say why it stays when they limit the change". Wants an eval before it ships (006).
- 009 lessons Observation 4: a test file that failed to load before a git-ignored build existed stays current FAIL after the build appears (no recorded read of the ignored path); nearest entry is 007's runtime reads.

- 007: observed closure growth outside `observe.runtimeInputs`: a pytest file whose run imports a path outside its static closure and outside the shared session key makes the result incomplete but does not grow the stored closure for the next key (007-14 option (b), 2026-10-14).
- 007: data files read at run time enter no pytest closure (spec 007 open question 2); an audit hook on `open` was measured in wave 1 and not shipped. A row once a dogfood shows a missed data-file edit.


- 012 (from 012-01, the human 2026-10-20): Cursor CLI imports the Claude Code plugin and drops each hook's `args`. Since 0.1.230 every command alone exits 0, except under a plugin root with whitespace. There the shell runs whatever file the root's first word names, which gets the payload on stdin (CC1's exception). A route through Cursor's own manifest would close it and would let Squeal work under Cursor at all: Cursor reads `.cursor-plugin/plugin.json` before `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` in a plugin directory (read in its 2026.10.16 source). Whether that manifest stops the import of our Claude hooks is unverified. Research first. With it, close `reviews/012-01-r2.md`'s note: the whitespace-root test asserts neither the "not found" line nor whether stdin was read.
- 012: a SubagentStop report restarts the subagent in Claude Code, so the parent receives the subagent's reply to the report instead of its answer to the task (005 `research/instruction-surfaces.md` 1, session `can-c`; 2.1.295). Probe first.

- 005 (from 001): `squeal` is not on a user's PATH after a plugin install: now spec 005 D3 (`install.sh`, the launcher, `squeal setup`; rows 005-14 and 005-19), decided by the human 2026-10-09.


- 011: `store.prune` removes a removed worktree's results but not its per-worktree `meta` rows (`slow-tier:<worktreeId>` from 004-15, `optimizer-off.<worktreeId>` from 001-181, `held-files:<worktreeId>` and `reruns:<worktreeId>` from 001-187 (`reviews/wave-13j.md` S4), `slow-artifacts:<worktreeId>` from 004-23, and `failure-keys:<worktreeId>` from 004-25 (newest 1,024 failing checks' keys, written by `src/core/state/sink.ts`), all built in `src/core/slow/state.ts`). Prune them with the worktree by those known prefixes (a registry of per-worktree prefixes), never by a `:<worktreeId>` suffix match: the shared `nodeTest.observed.<project>` and `nodeTest.observedPreloads.<project>` keys must survive, in the same short batches as 001-141. Reported by the 002/003/004 coordinator, 2026-10-09.
